float]:\n    finite = np.asarray(values, dtype=np.float32)\n    finite = finite[np.isfinite(finite)]\n    if finite.size == 0:\n        return 0.0, 0.0\n    q1, q2 = np.quantile(finite, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n    if q2 > q1:\n        return float(q1), float(q2)\n\n    sorted_vals = np.sort(finite)\n    i1 = max(0, min(sorted_vals.size - 1, sorted_vals.size // 3))\n    i2 = max(0, min(sorted_vals.size - 1, (2 * sorted_vals.size) // 3))\n    q1 = float(sorted_vals[i1])\n    q2 = float(sorted_vals[i2])\n    if q2 <= q1:\n        larger = sorted_vals[sorted_vals > q1]\n        q2 = float(larger[0]) if larger.size else q1\n    return q1, q2\n\n\ndef digitize_three_bins(values: np.ndarray, edges: tuple[float, float]) -> np.ndarray:\n    low, high = edges\n    if high <= low:\n        return np.zeros(values.shape, dtype=np.int8)\n    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array([low, high], dtype=np.float32), right=False).astype(np.int8)\n\n\ndef load_good_spikes_and_regions(spec: SessionSpec, brain_regions: BrainRegions) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    spike_times_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    spike_clusters_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    cluster_regions_all: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    cluster_offset = 0\n\n    for probe_name in spec.probe_names:\n        probe_dir = spec.session_path / \"alf\" / probe_name / \"pykilosort\"\n        metrics_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/clusters.metrics.pqt\")\n        clusters_channels_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/clusters.channels.npy\")\n        channels_region_ids_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy\")\n        spikes_times_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/spikes.times.npy\")\n        spikes_clusters_path = resolve_latest(probe_dir, \"**/spikes.clusters.npy\")\n\n        metrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_path, columns=[\"label\"])\n        good_rows = metrics[\"label\"].to_numpy(copy=False) >= 1\n        if not np.any(good_rows):\n            continue\n\n        spikes_times = np.load(spikes_times_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n        spikes_clusters = np.load(spikes_clusters_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n        n_clusters = good_rows.shape[0]\n        valid_spikes = spikes_clusters < n_clusters\n        if np.all(valid_spikes):\n            spike_mask = good_rows[spikes_clusters]\n        else:\n            spike_mask = np.zeros(spikes_clusters.shape[0], dtype=bool)\n            spike_mask[valid_spikes] = good_rows[spikes_clusters[valid_spikes]]\n        remap = np.full(n_clusters, -1, dtype=np.int32)\n        remap[good_rows] = np.arange(int(good_rows.sum()), dtype=np.int32)\n        good_spike_times = np.asarray(spikes_times[spike_mask], dtype=np.float64)\n        good_spike_clusters = remap[spikes_clusters[spike_mask]] + cluster_offset\n\n        cluster_channels = np.load(clusters_channels_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")[good_rows]\n        channel_region_ids = np.load(channels_region_ids_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n        region_ids = np.zeros(cluster_channels.shape[0], dtype=np.int64)\n        valid_channel = (cluster_channels >= 0) & (cluster_channels < channel_region_ids.shape[0])\n        region_ids[valid_channel] = channel_region_ids[cluster_channels[valid_channel]]\n        cluster_regions = brain_regions.id2acronym(region_ids, mapping=\"Beryl\").astype(str)\n\n        spike_times_all.append(good_spike_times)\n        spike_clusters_all.append(good_spike_clusters)\n        cluster_regions_all.append(cluster_regions)\n        cluster_offset += cluster_regions.shape[0]\n\n    if cluster_offset == 0:\n        raise ValueError(f\"No good units remained for {spec.eid}\")\n\n    merged_spike_times = np.concatenate(spike_times_all)\n    merged_spike_clusters = np.concatenate(spike_clusters_all)\n    order = np.argsort(merged_spike_times)\n    merged_spike_times = merged_spike_times[order]\n    merged_spike_clusters = merged_spike_clusters[order]\n    merged_cluster_regions = np.concatenate(cluster_regions_all)\n    return merged_spike_times, merged_spike_clusters, merged_cluster_regions\n\n\ndef bin_spikes_by_trial(\n    spike_times: np.ndarray,\n    spike_clusters: np.ndarray,\n    align_times: np.ndarray,\n    n_units: int,\n    binsize: float = BINSIZE,\n    time_window: tuple[float, float] = TIME_WINDOW,\n) -> list[np.ndarray]:\n    interval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]\n    interval_ends = align_times + time_window[1]\n    n_bins = int(np.ceil((time_window[1] - time_window[0]) / binsize))\n    start_idx = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_begs, side=\"left\")\n    end_idx = np.searchsorted(spike_times, interval_ends, side=\"left\")\n\n    out: list[np.ndarray] = []\n    for i in range(len(align_times)):\n        i0 = start_idx[i]\n        i1 = end_idx[i]\n        if i1 <= i0:\n            out.append(np.zeros((n_units, n_bins), dtype=np.float16))\n            continue\n\n        rel = spike_times[i0:i1] - interval_begs[i]\n        bin_idx = np.floor(rel / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n        valid = (bin_idx >= 0) & (bin_idx < n_bins)\n        if not np.any(valid):\n            out.append(np.zeros((n_units, n_bins), dtype=np.float16))\n            continue\n\n        flat = spike_clusters[i0:i1][valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\n        counts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_bins).reshape(n_units, n_bins)\n        out.append(counts.astype(np.float16))\n    return out\n\n\ndef map_choice(raw_choice: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapped = np.empty(raw_choice.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n    mapped[raw_choice == 1] = 0\n    mapped[raw_choice == -1] = 1\n    if not np.all(np.isin(raw_choice, [-1, 1])):\n        raise ValueError(\"Unexpected choice values encountered after filtering.\")\n    return mapped\n\n\ndef map_prior(raw_prior: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.empty(raw_prior.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n    mapper = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n    for i, val in enumerate(raw_prior):\n        key = round(float(val), 1)\n        if key not in mapper:\n            raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected probabilityLeft value {val}\")\n        out[i] = mapper[key]\n    return out\n\n\ndef build_session_payload(\n    prepared: PreparedSession,\n    wheel_edges: tuple[float, float],\n    whisker_edges: tuple[float, float],\n) -> tuple[str, list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], list[np.ndarray], np.ndarray, float]:\n    session_start = time.time()\n    brain_regions = BrainRegions()\n    kept_align_times = prepared.trials.loc[prepared.keep_mask, ALIGN_EVENT].to_numpy(dtype=np.float64)\n    spike_times, COMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES,\n                np.full(NBINS, prepared.trial_number_in_block[trial_idx], dtype=np.float32),\n            ]\n        ).astype(np.float32)\n        out = np.vstack(\n            [\n                np.full(NBINS, choice[trial_idx], dtype=np.int8),\n                np.full(NBINS, prior[trial_idx], dtype=np.int8),\n                wheel_bins[trial_idx],\n `code/ibllib` is the library dependency used to access IBL data.\n    51→- The reference pipeline is electrophysiology, not imaging. No delta-F/F computation is involved.\n    52→- Reference caching parameters in `0_data_caching.py`:\n    53→  - `align_time='stimOn_times'`\n    54→  - `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds\n    55→  - `interval_len=2`\n    56→  - `binsize=0.02` seconds\n    57→- Therefore the nominal neural/behavior trial window is 2 s around stimulus onset, with 20 ms bins, yielding 100 bins.\n    58→- Trial curation in `load_trials_and_mask` uses:\n    59→  - reaction time between 0.08 s and 2.0 s\n    60→  - trial duration (`feedback_times - goCue_times`) <= 10 s\n    61→  - non-null `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`\n    62→  - exclude no-choice trials (`choice == 0`)\n    63→  - do not exclude unbiased block by default\n    64→- Spike processing:\n    65→  - probes are merged within session\n    66→  - all clusters are loaded during caching (`qc=None`)\n    67→  - cluster QC is still recorded in metadata as `good_clusters = (label >= 1)`\n    68→  - no firing-rate smoothing; data are spike counts per bin\n    69→- Behavior processing:\n    70→  - `choice`, `block` (`probabilityLeft`), `reward`, and signed `contrast` are per-trial values\n    71→  - `wheel-speed` is `abs(wheel velocity)`\n    72→  - `whisker-motion-energy` prefers left camera and falls back to right camera if needed\n    73→  - continuous streams are linearly interpolated within each trial-aligned window\n    74→  - interpolation sample points are `linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`\n    75→- Data orientation in the cached dataset is `(trial, time, neuron)` for spikes; decoder target format will need per-trial matrices transposed to `(neuron, time)`.\n    76→- Potential discrepancy to revisit later: `align_spike_behavior` uses Python list `and` semantics and overwrites `beh_mask` within the loop, so the effective trial filtering logic may only reflect the last behavior plus `trials_mask`.\n 160→- Task structure from paper:\n   161→  - visual stimulus left/right; mouse reports by wheel turn\n   162→  - 90 unbiased trials at 50:50, then alternating biased blocks with `probabilityLeft` 0.2 or 0.8\n   163→  - 0% contrast trials follow block prior and still require a left/right response\n   164→- Temporal alignment from method paper:\n   165→  - choice: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.5 s to +1.5 s\n   166→  - prior: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.6 s to -0.1 s\n   167→  - wheel speed / whisker motion energy: align to first movement onset, decode from alignment event to +1.0 s\n   168→- Temporal binning from method paper:\n   169→  - 20 ms bins for choice and dynamic behaviors in the main formulation\n   170→  - 50 ms bins specifically stated for prior\n   171→- Additional decoding details from data paper:\n   172→  - wheel speed / velocity decoded in non-overlapping 20 ms bins from -0.2 s to +1.0 s around first movement, with a causal window of W = 10 bins for regression\n   173→  - binary variables evaluated with balanced accuracy\n   174→  - continuous variables evaluated with R2\n   175→- Motion energy definition from data paper:\n   176→  - mean absolute pixel difference between adjacent frames in a whisker-pad bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye\n   177→- Data architecture white paper:\n   178→  - raw data are stored in ALF / ONE-style dataset types\n   179→  - session-relative files and revisions are expected; this matches the versioned `alf/#...#/` files observed locally\n   180→\n   181→### Curation Steps\n   182→\n   183→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   184→- Public release kept 621,733 units including multineuron activity.\n   185→- Main data-paper analyses further defined “well-isolated neurons” using RIGOR single-unit QC:\n   186→  - amplitude > 50 uV\n   187→  - noise cut-off < 20 uV\n   188→  - refractory-period violation criterion\n   189→- Final main analyses further restricted to grey-matter regions with at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least 2 such sessions.\n   190→\n   191→**Trial curation rules**:\n   192→- Release / analysis trial exclusions in data paper:\n   193→  - exclude trials missing `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, or `firstMovement_times`\n   194→  - exclude trials with reaction time (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) outside 0.08–2.00 s\n   195→- Sessions in public release had to satisfy:\n   196→  - at least 250 trials\n   197→  - at least 90% correct on 100% contrast trials for both left and right blocks\n   198→  - at least 3 incorrect-choice trials after exclusions\n   199→  - hardware QC threshold\n   200→- Main paper also notes only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses.\n   201→\n   202→### Decoders Trained\n   203→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   204→| Choice | multi-session behavioral-state model improves over baseline; example figure shows choice AUC improving from about 0.66 baseline to about 0.79 on an example session |\n   205→| Prior | LG-AR1 improves over baseline; example figure shows prior correlation improving from about 0.05 baseline to about 0.34 multi-session and about 0.65 oracle on an example session |\n   206→| Wheel speed | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n   207→| Whisker motion energy | paper evaluates with R2; figures indicate multi-session RRR outperforms baseline / single-session models |\n   208→\n Use a common 20 ms stimulus-onset grid for the converted dataset, and document that this is a task-imposed simplification relative to the method paper’s prior-specific window. |\n   224→| Temporal alignment for `wheel-speed` and `whisker-motion-energy` | provided code cache aligns all behaviors to `stimOn_times` | raw data provide continuous wheel and whisker streams plus `stimOn_times` and `firstMovement_times` | method paper says dynamic behaviors should align to `firstMovement_times` and run to +1 s | User explicitly requires “Temporally align based on stimulus onset.” Therefore, use stimulus-onset alignment for dynamic outputs as a deliberate task override, while keeping the reference code’s binning, interpolation style, and trial masking wherever possible. |\n   225→| Binning of dynamic behavior | code uses 20 ms bins and linear interpolation inside each interval | raw wheel / motion-energy streams have irregular session-wide sampling (wheel) or camera-rate sampling (60 Hz / 150 Hz) | method paper dynamic behavior bins are 20 ms | No substantive discrepancy. Follow ALF / ONE naming and revision handling already present in local cache. |\n   229→\n   230→Final understanding:\n   231→- Start from the frozen 459-session / 699-insertion BWM release, not from all locally visible session folders.\n   232→- Apply the shared trial-quality filters from the papers plus the provided code’s `exclude_nochoice=True` and `max_trial_len=10.0`.\n   233→- Use `label >= 1` well-isolated units because this reproduces the paper’s 75,708-neuron statistic exactly and is the release-consistent dense representation that remains computationally tractable for the provided decoder.\n   234→- Use a common 20 ms stimulus-onset-aligned grid for the converted dataset because the user explicitly requires stimulus-onset alignment and the target decoder needs common trial shapes across neural, input, and output streams.\n   235→- Treat method-paper target-specific alignment differences (especially dynamic behaviors and prior) as important reference context, but as overridden where they conflict with the user’s explicit decoder specification.\n   236→\n   237→---\n   238→\n   239→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   240→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   241→\n   242→### 244→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   245→| `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters`, `clusters.metrics.label` from all probes in a frozen-release `probabilityLeft` block structure in raw trial table | `input[1]` = `trial_number_in_block` | compute block index from original unfiltered session trial sequence; reset to 1 whenever `probabilityLeft` changes; then repeat the scalar across all 100 bins for each kept trial | custom logic consistent with raw trial table semantics | unbiased 0.5 block counts as its own block |\n   248→| raw `choice` in trial table (`-1`, `1`) after filtering | `output[0]` = `choice` | map left `1 -> 0`, right `-1 -> 1`; repeat across all 100 bins for a common 2D output shape | `load_trials_and_mask`, `bin_behaviors` | verified directly from high-contrast correct trials; no-choice trials are removed before mapping |\n   249→| raw `probabilityLeft` in trial table (`0.2`, `0.5`, `0.8`) | `output[1]` = `prior_probability_of_left` | map `0.2 -> 0`, `0.5 -> 1`, `0.8 -> 2`; repeat across all 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` for `block` source; task-specific remapping | categorical per trial |\n   250→| wheel trace from `_ibl_wheel.*` via ONE/SessionLoader-equivalent logic | `output[2]` = `wheel_speed_bin` | compute wheel speed as absolute velocity; align/interpolate to the common stimulus-onset grid; discretize using global 3-bin thresholds; store category index `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | global thresholds shared across all sessions/trials for consistent categories |\n   251→| whisker motion energy from left camera, else right camera | `output[3]` = `whisker_motion_energy_bin` | load left whisker motion energy if available, else right; align/interpolate to common stimulus-onset grid; discretize with global 3-bin thresholds; store `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior`, `bin_behaviors`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | do not mix left/right within a trial; session uses preferred available side |\n   252→| subject IDs from frozen release metadata | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique sorted subject list and per-session index | `bwm_release.csv` session metadata | order matches session order in converted dataset |\n   253→| cluster acronyms from probe cluster metadata | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | map each neuron acronym to Beryl acronym, build unique region list, store integer region index per neuron | `list_brain_regions` / `BrainRegions().acronym2acronym(..., mapping='Beryl')` | Beryl mapping matches reference region aggregation used in code |\n   254→| release/session metadata | `metadata` | include task description, 20 ms bin size, stimulus-onset alignment, offsets `-0.5/+1.5`, release source, discretization thresholds, modality notes | custom | keep enough detail for reproducibility |\n   255→\n   256→### Key Decisions\n   257→1. **Use the 459-session frozen BWM release as the source session set**: This matches both the data paper public release and `code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv`.\n   258→1. **Require local existence of trials, wheel, and at least one whisker motion-energy stream**: All 459 frozen sessions exist locally; 439 have at least one whisker motion-energy stream, so sessions lacking whisker motion energy will be excluded because that output is mandatory for this decoder task.\n   259→1. **Use a single stimulus-onset-aligned 20 ms grid for every variable**: This satisfies the user’s explicit task requirement and simplifies the validator/decoder interface.\n   260→1. **Represent both inputs as 2D arrays of shape `(2, 100)`**: `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` varies over time; `trial_number_in_block` is repeated across time for each trial.\n   261→1. **Represent all outputs as 2D arrays of shape `(4, 100)`**: static outputs (`choice`, `prior`) are repeated across time so all outputs share a common structure with the dynamic outputs.\n   262→1. **Filter neurons to `label >= 1`, while preserving per-neuron QC metadata and Beryl region labels**: This matches the public-release well-isolated-neuron count of 75,708 exactly and keeps the dense pickle size compatible with the provided decoder.\n   263→1. **Compute `trial_number_in_block` on the original trial table before filtering**: This preserves the actual behavioural position within a block rather than renumbering after trial exclusion.\n   264→1. **Use left whisker motion energy when present, else right**: This matches the provided code and maximizes session retention.\n   265→1. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy with global 3-bin thresholds**: session-specific thresholds would make class labels inconsistent across sessions; global thresholds keep categories comparable.\n   266→1. **Drop any trial lacking full valid coverage of neural, wheel, or whisker data on the common window**: no padding or fabricated values.\n   267→\n   268→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   269→- [ ] Neural spot-check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron, recompute binned spike counts directly from raw `spikes.times` / `spikes.clusters` and verify equality with `np.allclose()`.\n   270→- [ ] Input spot-check: verify the converted `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` row equals the planned 100-bin relative-time vector and that `trial_number_in_block` matches direct computation from the raw trial table.\n   271→- [ ] Output spot-check (static): verify converted `choice` and `prior_probability_of_left` categories match raw `choice` and `probabilityLeft` on selected trials.\n   272→- [ ] Output spot-check (dynamic): verify a selected trial’s wheel-speed and whisker-motion-energy category sequences match direct raw interpolation followed by bin assignment using the saved global thresholds.\n   273→- [ ] Session-count sanity: compare number of included sessions and trials after mandatory modality/trial filtering against the 459-session release and the 433-session method-paper analysis subset.\n   274→- [ ] Neuron-count sanity: compare converted neuron counts both before and after optional QC summaries against the raw cluster counts and the paper’s well-isolated-neuron count.\n   275→\n   276→---\n   277→\n   278→## Step 6: Script Development\n   279→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   280→\n   281→- Installed `iblatlas` successfully so Beryl region mapping can match the reference code.\n   282→- Direct reuse of `brainbox.io.one.SessionLoader` is not viable in this environment because `brainbox.io.one` imports the unavailable `neuropixel` package, and `ONE.load_object(...)` against the local cache hits `.rest` permission issues.\n   283→- Conversion will therefore use direct ALF file loading plus the bundled `brainbox.behavior.wheel.interpolate_position` / `velocity_filtered` helpers and the bundled `brainbox.population.decode` logic where possible.\n   284→- Verified that filtering clusters by `label >= 1` across the 699 frozen-release insertions yields exactly 75,708 units, matching the data paper.\n   285→- Implemented `/app/convert_data.py` as a two-pass converter:\n   286→  - pass 1 loads trials + wheel + whisker, applies the reference trial mask plus behavior-coverage mask, and computes global dynamic-output tertile thresholds\n   287→  - pass 2 reloads good units (`label >= 1`), bins stimulus-aligned spikes into 20 ms bins, constructs decoder inputs/outputs, and writes the pickle\n   288→- Implemented direct Beryl region mapping from `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy` via `iblatlas.regions.BrainRegions`.\n   289→- Implemented reference-style video timestamp repair where camera timestamps longer than motion-energy arrays are trimmed from the front.\n   290→- Smoke test completed successfully:\n   291→  - command: `python3 -u /app/convert_data.py /app/_step6_smoke.pkl --sample`\n   292→  - result: 2 sessions, 651 trials, 340 well-isolated neurons, no runtime errors\n   293→\n   294→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   295→- Repeated recursive path discovery across revisioned ALF files would be slow if done inside inner trial loops.\n   296→- Dense float32 storage would be too large for the full release.\n   297→- Initial full-run profiling showed the real bottleneck was spike-stream reload and dtype copying in pass 2, not the trial-binning code.\n   298→\n   299→Code speedups added:\n   300→- Vectorized per-trial spike binning via `np.searchsorted` + flattened `np.bincount`.\n   301→- Low-precision storage for neural arrays (`float16`) to keep the dense pickle tractable while remaining compatible with the decoder validator.\n   302→- Behavior interpolation performed only once per session in pass 1; neural binning deferred until after global discretization thresholds are known.\n   303→- Session-level parallelism for full runs via `ThreadPoolExecutor`; sample / plotting runs stay single-worker for simpler debugging.\n   304→- BLAS/OpenMP thread counts are capped at 1 inside the converter so session-level parallelism is not drowned out by nested threading.\n   305→- Pass-2 spike loading now uses ALF-native cluster indexing, memory-mapped spike arrays, and no-copy dtype handling where possible; this removed the largest full-array copies.\n   306→- Merged spike times no longer use stable sort because within-timestamp probe ordering is irrelevant for binned counts.\n   307→- Full-run worker cap increased to 32 after confirming the machine has 64 CPUs and ample RAM.\n   308→\n   309→---\n   310→\n   311→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   312→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   313→\n   314→### Sample `code/ibllib` is the library dependency used to access IBL data.\n- The reference pipeline is electrophysiology, not imaging. No delta-F/F computation is involved.\n- Reference caching parameters in `0_data_caching.py`:\n  - `align_time='stimOn_times'`\n  - `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds\n  - `interval_len=2`\n  - `binsize=0.02` seconds\n- Therefore the nominal neural/behavior trial window is 2 s around stimulus onset, with 20 ms bins, yielding 100 bins.\n- Trial curation in `load_trials_and_mask` uses:\n  - reaction time between 0.08 s and 2.0 s\n  - trial duration (`feedback_times - goCue_times`) <= 10 s\n  - non-null `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`\n  - exclude no-choice trials (`choice == 0`)\n  - do not exclude unbiased block by default\n- Spike processing:\n  - probes are merged within session\n  - all clusters are loaded during caching (`qc=None`)\n  - cluster QC is still recorded in metadata as `good_clusters = (label >= 1)`\n  - no firing-rate smoothing; data are spike counts per bin\n- Behavior processing:\n  - `choice`, `block` (`probabilityLeft`), `reward`, and signed `contrast` are per-trial values\n  - `wheel-speed` is `abs(wheel velocity)`\n  - `whisker-motion-energy` prefers left camera and falls back to right camera if needed\n  - continuous streams are linearly interpolated within each trial-aligned window\n  - interpolation sample points are `linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`\n- Data orientation in the cached dataset is `(trial, time, neuron)` for spikes; decoder target format will need per-trial matrices transposed to `(neuron, time)`.\n- Potential discrepancy to revisit later: `align_spike_behavior` uses Python list `and` semantics and overwrites `beh_mask` within the loop, so the effective trial filtering logic may only reflect the last behavior plus `trials_mask`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- Main raw data cache is under `data/one_cache/`.\n- Three registry snapshots are present:\n  - mouse reports by wheel turn\n  - 90 unbiased trials at 50:50, then alternating biased blocks with `probabilityLeft` 0.2 or 0.8\n  - 0% contrast trials follow block prior and still require a left/right response\n- Temporal alignment from method paper:\n  - choice: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.5 s to +1.5 s\n  - prior: align to stimulus onset, use neural activity from -0.6 s to -0.1 s\n  - wheel speed / whisker motion energy: align to first movement onset, decode from alignment event to +1.0 s\n- Temporal binning from method paper:\n  - 20 ms bins for choice and dynamic behaviors in the main formulation\n  - 50 ms bins specifically stated for prior\n- Additional decoding details from data paper:\n  - wheel speed / velocity decoded in non-overlapping 20 ms bins from -0.2 s to +1.0 s around first movement, with a causal window of W = 10 bins for regression\n  - binary variables evaluated with balanced accuracy\n  - continuous variables evaluated with R2\n- Motion energy definition from data paper:\n  - mean absolute pixel difference between adjacent frames in a whisker-pad bounding box anchored between nose tip and eye\n- Data architecture white paper:\n  - raw data are stored in ALF / ONE-style dataset types\n  - session-relative files and revisions are expected; this matches the versioned `alf/#...#/` files observed locally\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Public release kept 621,733 units including multineuron activity.\n- Main data-paper analyses further defined “well-isolated neurons” using RIGOR single-unit QC:\n  - amplitude > 50 uV\n  - noise cut-off < 20 uV\n  - refractory-period violation criterion\n- Final main analyses further restricted to grey-matter regions with at least 5 well-isolated neurons per session and at least 2 such sessions.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Release / analysis trial exclusions in data paper:\n  - exclude trials missing `choice`, `probabilityLeft`, `feedbackType`, `feedback_times`, `stimOn_times`, or `firstMovement_times`\n  - exclude trials with reaction time (`firstMovement_times - stimOn_times`) outside 0.08–2.00 s\n- Sessions in public release had to satisfy:\n  - at least 250 trials\n  - at least 90% correct on 100% contrast trials for both left and right blocks\n  - at least 3 incorrect-choice trials after exclusions\n  - hardware QC threshold\n- Main paper also notes only sessions with at least 400 trials were retained for further analyses.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| Choice | multi-session behavioral-state model improves over baseline; example figure shows choice AUC improving from about 0.66 baseline to about 0.79 on an example session |\n| Prior | LG-AR1 improves over baseline; | `output[2]` = `wheel_speed_bin` | compute wheel speed as absolute velocity; align/interpolate to the common stimulus-onset grid; discretize using global 3-bin thresholds; store category index `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior('wheel-speed')`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | global thresholds shared across all sessions/trials for consistent categories |\n| whisker motion energy from left camera, else right camera | `output[3]` = `whisker_motion_energy_bin` | load left whisker motion energy if available, else right; align/interpolate to common stimulus-onset grid; discretize with global 3-bin thresholds; store `0/1/2` per time bin | `load_target_behavior`, `bin_behaviors`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | do not mix left/right within a trial; session uses preferred available converted dataset |\n| cluster acronyms from probe cluster metadata | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | map each neuron acronym to Beryl acronym, build unique region list, store integer region `metadata` | include task description, 20 ms bin size, stimulus-onset alignment, offsets `-0.5/+1.5`, release source, discretization thresholds, modality notes | custom | keep enough detail for reproducibility |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Use the 459-session frozen BWM release as the source session set**: This matches both the data paper public release and `code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv`.\n1. **Require local existence of trials, wheel, and at least one whisker motion-energy stream**: All 459 frozen sessions exist locally; 439 have at least one whisker motion-energy stream, so sessions lacking whisker motion energy will be excluded because that output is mandatory for this decoder task.\n1. **Use a single stimulus-onset-aligned 20 ms grid for every variable**: This satisfies the user’s explicit task requirement and simplifies the validator/decoder interface.\n1. **Represent both inputs as 2D arrays of shape `(2, 100)`**: `time_since_stimulus_onset_s` varies over time; `trial_number_in_block` is repeated across time for each trial.\n1. **Represent all outputs as 2D arrays of shape `(4, 100)`**: static outputs (`choice`, `prior`) are repeated across time so all outputs share a common structure with the dynamic outputs.\n1. **Filter neurons to `label >= 1`, while preserving per-neuron QC metadata and Beryl region labels**: This matches the public-release well-isolated-neuron count of 75,708 exactly and keeps the dense pickle size compatible with the provided decoder.\n1. **Compute `trial_number_in_block` on the original trial table before filtering**: This preserves the actual behavioural position within a block rather than renumbering after trial exclusion.\n1. **Use left whisker motion energy when present, else right**: This matches the provided code and maximizes session retention.\n1. **Discretize wheel speed and whisker motion energy with global 3-bin thresholds**: session-specific thresholds would make class labels inconsistent across sessions; global thresholds keep categories comparable.\n1. **Drop any trial lacking full valid coverage of neural, wheel, or whisker data on the common window**: no padding or fabricated values.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Neural spot-check: for a chosen session/trial/neuron, recompute binned spike counts directly The AI groups sessions by subject and builds a sorted unique subject list. `subject_idx` maps each session to its index in this sorted list.\n\nii.\n```python\nsubject_names = sorted({ps.spec.subject for ps in prepared_sessions})\nsubject_to_idx = {subject: i for i, subject in enumerate(subject_names)}\n```\n\niii. The subject name comes directly from the release CSV metadata, consistent with the reference approach of deriving subjects from session metadata.\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. Each row (grouped by `eid`) in `bwm_release.csv` corresponds to one session. The AI groups the CSV by `eid` and creates one `SessionSpec` per unique session.\n\nii.\n```python\ngrouped = bwm.groupby(\"eid\", sort=False)\nfor eid, df in grouped:\n    sessions.append(SessionSpec(eid=eid, ...))\n```\n\niii. Sessions are already the unit of organization in the release CSV; no splitting is needed.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. The trials table (`_ibl_trials.table.pqt`) has one row per trial; this defines the trial split.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_trials_table(session_path: Path) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    path = resolve_latest(session_path / \"alf\", \"**/_ibl_trials.table.pqt\")\n    return pd.read_parquet(path)\n```\n\niii. No decision to make; trials are already one row per trial in the data.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies several filters: (1) non-null `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`; (2) reaction time between 0.08 and 2.0 s; (3) trial duration (`feedback_times - goCue_times`) <= 10 s; (4) `choice != 0` (exclude no-choice); (5) wheel and whisker coverage of the trial window. This is more filters than the reference, which does not check `feedbackType` non-null or `max_trial_len`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_trial_mask(trials: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:\n    rt = trials[\"firstMovement_times\"] - trials[\"stimOn_times\"]\n    mask = (\n        ~trials[\"stimOn_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"choice\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"feedback_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"probabilityLeft\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"firstMovement_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"feedbackType\"].isnull()\n        & (rt >= 0.08)\n        & (rt <= 2.0)\n        & ((trials[\"feedback_times\"] - trials[\"goCue_times\"]) <= 10.0)\n        & (trials[\"choice\"] != 0)\n    )\n    return mask.to_numpy(dtype=bool)\n```\n\niii. The AI derived its trial mask from the reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` function, which includes the `max_trial_len=10.0` and `feedbackType` non-null checks. The AI documented this as \"keeping `exclude_nochoice=True` and `max_trial_len=10.0` to remain consistent with the provided code path.\"\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Two arrays per probe: `spikes.times.npy` and `spikes.clusters.npy`. The cluster quality label comes from `clusters.metrics.pqt` and brain regions from `clusters.channels.npy` and `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy`.\n\nii.\n```python\nspikes_times = np.load(spikes_times_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\nspikes_clusters = np.load(spikes_clusters_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n```\n\niii. These are the standard IBL spike sorting outputs, matching the reference.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are counted into 20 ms bins over the [-0.5, 1.5] s trial window relative to stimulus onset. However, the AI stores **raw spike counts** (as float16), NOT firing rates. The reference divides by BIN (0.02) to convert to Hz. The AI also uses float16 precision rather than float32.\n\nii.\n```python\nflat = spike_clusters[i0:i1][valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\ncounts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_bins).reshape(n_units, n_bins)\nout.append(counts.astype(np.float16))\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES mention \"no firing-rate smoothing; data are spike counts per bin\" from the reference code analysis, but the AI appears to have missed that the reference actually converts counts to rates by dividing by the bin width.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only clusters with `label >= 1` in `clusters.metrics.pqt` are kept. The AI verified this yields exactly 75,708 units across the full 459-session release, matching the data paper.\n\nii.\n```python\nmetrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_path, columns=[\"label\"])\ngood_rows = metrics[\"label\"].to_numpy(copy=False) >= 1\n```\n\niii. This matches the data paper's definition of \"well-isolated neurons\" and reproduces the reported count of 75,708.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to stimulus onset (`stimOn_times`). Spike times are binned relative to the start of the trial window (`align_time + T_START`), which puts zero at stimulus onset time.\n\nii.\n```python\nALIGN_EVENT = \"stimOn_times\"\n...\ninterval_begs = align_times + time_window[0]  # align_time - 0.5\n...\nrel = spike_times[i0:i1] - interval_begs[i]\nbin_idx = np.floor(rel / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The AI noted the user explicitly requires \"Temporally align based on stimulus onset.\"\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. 20 ms bins, 100 bins per trial over the 2 s window. No rebinning is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nBINSIZE = 0.02\nNBINS = int(round((TIME_WINDOW[1] - TIME_WINDOW[0]) / BINSIZE))  # 100\n```\n\niii. Matches the reference code's `binsize=0.02` and the method paper's description of \"20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps.\"\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time since stimulus onset* derived from?\n\ni. From `stimOn_times` in the trials table, which defines the alignment event. The time input is a fixed vector of time points relative to stimulus onset.\n\nii.\n```python\nCOMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], NBINS, dtype=np.float32)\n# = np.linspace(-0.48, 1.5, 100)\n```\n\niii. The AI uses the same time grid construction as the reference code's `get_behavior_per_interval` function.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time since stimulus onset*?\n\ni. The time vector is computed as `np.linspace(-0.48, 1.5, 100)`, which produces right bin edges rather than bin centers. The reference uses bin centers: `EDGES[:-1] + BIN/2 = [-0.49, -0.47, ..., 1.49]`.\n\nii.\n```python\nCOMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], NBINS, dtype=np.float32)\n```\n\nReference uses:\n```python\nTIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2  # bin centres\n```\n\niii. The AI followed the reference code's `get_behavior_per_interval` which uses `np.linspace(interval_beg + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)` for interpolation points. This is slightly shifted from the bin centers used by the reference human solution.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time since stimulus onset* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The same `COMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES` vector is used as both the time input and the interpolation grid for behavioral signals. However, neural spike binning uses bins defined by `np.floor(rel / binsize)`, which defines bins from their left edges. The time input values are the right edges of these bins, not their centers.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Neural binning: floor division gives bin index from left edge\nbin_idx = np.floor(rel / binsize).astype(np.int64)\n\n# Time input: right edges\nCOMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], NBINS)\n```\n\niii. There is a subtle misalignment: the time input represents right bin edges while the neural bins are defined by left edges. The bin center would be more appropriate. However, this is consistent with how the reference code's `get_behavior_per_interval` defines its interpolation points.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number in block* derived from?\n\ni. From `probabilityLeft` in the trials table. Block boundaries are detected where `probabilityLeft` changes value.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_trial_number_in_block(prob_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    ...\n    for i, val in enumerate(prob_left):\n        current = None if pd.isna(val) else float(val)\n        if i == 0 or current != prev:\n            counter = 1\n        else:\n            counter += 1\n        out[i] = counter\n        prev = current\n    return out\n```\n\niii. The blocks are recovered from changes in `probabilityLeft`, consistent with the reference approach.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number in block*?\n\ni. The AI counts trials within each block starting from **1**, whereas the reference starts from **0** (using `pandas.groupby.cumcount()`). The count is computed on the unfiltered trial table before trial quality filtering, so excluded trials still advance the counter.\n\nii.\n```python\n# AI: starts at 1\ncounter = 1\n...\ncounter += 1\n\n# Reference: starts at 0\ntrial_in_block = trials.groupby(block).cumcount()\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES do not explicitly discuss the choice to start from 1 vs 0. The reference uses `cumcount()` which produces 0-indexed values.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Choice* derived from?\n\ni. From the `choice` column in the trials table, which takes values +1 (left), -1 (right), and 0 (no response).\n\nii.\n```python\nchoice_raw = trials.loc[keep_mask, \"choice\"].to_numpy()\n```\n\niii. Standard IBL choice encoding.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Choice*?\n\ni. Choice is mapped: left (+1) -> 0, right (-1) -> 1. No-choice trials (0) are excluded by the trial mask.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef map_choice(raw_choice: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapped = np.empty(raw_choice.shape[0], dtype=np.int8)\n    mapped[raw_choice == 1] = 0\n    mapped[raw_choice == -1] = 1\n    return mapped\n```\n\niii. Matches the instruction specification: \"left = 0, right = 1.\"\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Prior probability of left* derived from?\n\ni. From `probabilityLeft` in the trials table, which takes values 0.2, 0.5, and 0.8.\n\nii.\n```python\nprior_raw = trials.loc[keep_mask, \"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy()\n```\n\niii. Standard IBL block prior variable.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Prior probability of left*?\n\ni. Mapped to categorical: 0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2. Values are rounded to 1 decimal place before mapping.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef map_prior(raw_prior: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    mapper = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n    for i, val in enumerate(raw_prior):\n        key = round(float(val), 1)\n        out[i] = mapper[key]\n    return out\n```\n\niii. Matches the instruction specification: \"0.2 -> 0, 0.5 -> 1, 0.8 -> 2.\"\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Wheel speed* derived from?\n\ni. From `_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy` and `_ibl_wheel.position.npy`. The position is interpolated to 1000 Hz and differentiated with a 20 Hz Butterworth low-pass filter to get velocity; speed is the absolute value.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_wheel_speed(session_path: Path) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    timestamps = np.load(resolve_latest(alf_path, \"**/_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy\"))\n    position = np.load(resolve_latest(alf_path, \"**/_ibl_wheel.position.npy\"))\n    interp_pos, interp_times = interpolate_position(timestamps, position, freq=1000)\n    velocity, _ = velocity_filtered(interp_pos, fs=1000, corner_frequency=20, order=8)\n    return interp_times.astype(np.float64), np.abs(velocity).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The AI directly imports and calls the same `interpolate_position` and `velocity_filtered` functions from `brainbox.behavior.wheel`, matching the reference processing. The filter order is 8 (matching the default in `velocity_filtered`).\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Wheel speed*?\n\ni. The continuous wheel speed trace is interpolated onto the trial-aligned time grid using `np.interp`. It is then discretized into 3 bins. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES state: \"session-specific thresholds would make class labels inconsistent across sessions; global thresholds keep categories comparable.\" This is a deliberate design choice that differs from the reference's per-session approach.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Wheel speed* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Global tertile edges are computed using `np.quantile` at [1/3, 2/3] across all kept timepoints from all sessions. Values are then digitized into 3 bins (0, 1, 2) using `np.digitize`. The AI also includes a `robust_tertile_edges` fallback for degenerate cases.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef robust_tertile_edges(values):\n    q1, q2 = np.quantile(finite, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n    ...\n    return float(q1), float(q2)\n\ndef digitize_three_bins(values, edges):\n    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array([low, high]), right=False).astype(np.int8)\n```\n\niii. The reference uses per-session percentiles at [100/3, 200/3] which guarantees roughly equal-sized classes within each session. The AI's global approach does not guarantee equal class sizes per session.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Wheel speed* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The trials table (`_ibl_trials.table.pqt`) has one row per trial; this defines the trial split.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef load_trials_table(session_path: Path) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    path = resolve_latest(session_path / \"alf\", \"**/_ibl_trials.table.pqt\")\n    return pd.read_parquet(path)\n```\n\niii. No decision to make; trials are already one row per trial in the data.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies several filters: (1) non-null `stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`; (2) reaction time between 0.08 and 2.0 s; (3) trial duration (`feedback_times - goCue_times`) <= 10 s; (4) `choice != 0` (exclude no-choice); (5) wheel and whisker coverage of the trial window. This is more filters than the reference, which does not check `feedbackType` non-null or `max_trial_len`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_trial_mask(trials: pd.DataFrame) -> np.ndarray:\n    rt = trials[\"firstMovement_times\"] - trials[\"stimOn_times\"]\n    mask = (\n        ~trials[\"stimOn_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"choice\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"feedback_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"probabilityLeft\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"firstMovement_times\"].isnull()\n        & ~trials[\"feedbackType\"].isnull()\n        & (rt >= 0.08)\n        & (rt <= 2.0)\n        & ((trials[\"feedback_times\"] - trials[\"goCue_times\"]) <= 10.0)\n        & (trials[\"choice\"] != 0)\n    )\n    return mask.to_numpy(dtype=bool)\n```\n\niii. The AI derived its trial mask from the reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` function, which includes the `max_trial_len=10.0` and `feedbackType` non-null checks. The AI documented this as \"keeping `exclude_nochoice=True` and `max_trial_len=10.0` to remain consistent with the provided code path.\"\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Two arrays per probe: `spikes.times.npy` and `spikes.clusters.npy`. The cluster quality label comes from `clusters.metrics.pqt` and brain regions from `clusters.channels.npy` and `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy`.\n\nii.\n```python\nspikes_times = np.load(spikes_times_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\nspikes_clusters = np.load(spikes_clusters_path, mmap_mode=\"r\")\n```\n\niii. These are the standard IBL spike sorting outputs, matching the reference.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are counted into 20 ms bins over the [-0.5, 1.5] s trial window relative to stimulus onset. However, the AI stores **raw spike counts** (as float16), NOT firing rates. The reference divides by BIN (0.02) to convert to Hz. The AI also uses float16 precision rather than float32.\n\nii.\n```python\nflat = spike_clusters[i0:i1][valid] * n_bins + bin_idx[valid]\ncounts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=n_units * n_bins).reshape(n_units, n_bins)\nout.append(counts.astype(np.float16))\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES mention \"no firing-rate smoothing; data are spike counts per bin\" from the reference code analysis, but the AI appears to have missed that the reference actually converts counts to rates by dividing by the bin width.\n\n## 2-c. The AI verified this yields exactly 75,708 units across the full 459-session release, matching the data paper.\n\nii.\n```python\nmetrics = pd.read_parquet(metrics_path, columns=[\"label\"])\ngood_rows = metrics[\"label\"].to_numpy(copy=False) >= 1\n```\n\niii. The time input is a fixed vector of time points relative to stimulus onset.\n\nii.\n```python\nCOMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], NBINS, dtype=np.float32)\n# = np.linspace(-0.48, 1.5, 100)\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time since stimulus onset*?\n\ni. The reference uses bin centers: `EDGES[:-1] + BIN/2 = [-0.49, -0.47, ..., 1.49]`.\n\nii.\n```python\nCOMMON_RELATIVE_TIMES = np.linspace(TIME_WINDOW[0] + BINSIZE, TIME_WINDOW[1], NBINS, dtype=np.float32)\n```\n\nReference uses:\n```python\nTIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2  # bin centres\n```\n\niii. This is slightly shifted from the bin centers used by the reference human solution.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time since stimulus onset* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni.
